One-term Republican [[Tim Scott]] was appointed in 2013 and won election to his first full term in [[2016 United States Senate election in South Carolina|2016]] with 60.6% of the vote. He said that while he ran for re-election in 2022, it would be his last time.<ref name="the hill august 9">{{cite news |first=Rachel |last=Frazin |date=August 9, 2019 |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/456876-gop-sen-tim-scott-says-if-he-runs-in-2022-it-will-be-his-last-race |title=GOP Sen. Tim Scott says if he runs in 2022 it will be his last race |newspaper=The Hill}}</ref> In the Democratic primary, [[South Carolina House of Representatives|state representative]] [[Krystle Matthews]] defeated author and activist [[Catherine Fleming Bruce]]<ref>{{Cite news |last=Shain |first=Andy |date=January 17, 2022 |title="Columbia preservationist joins US Senate race for Tim Scott's seat" |work=The Post and Courier |url=https://www.postandcourier.com/columbia/politics/columbia-preservationist-joins-us-senate-race-for-tim-scotts-seat/article_4c84cbb2-77a0-11ec-aa29-c7b9f857ae60.html |access-date=November 10, 2022}}</ref> in a runoff.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Novelly |first1=Thomas |date=April 12, 2021 |title=Lowcountry Democratic lawmaker announces challenge to Republican Sen. Tim Scott in 2022 |url=https://www.postandcourier.com/politics/lowcountry-democratic-lawmaker-announces-challenge-to-republican-sen-tim-scott-in-2022/article_33132282-9baf-11eb-940e-972d7bbee7a2.html |access-date=April 13, 2021 |website=Post and Courier |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Jabour |first=Tara |date=June 22, 2022 |title="SC Representative Krystle Matthews faces Catherine Fleming Bruce in a runoff election" |work=WCIV-TV |url=https://abcnews4.com/news/local/sc-representative-krystle-matthews-faces-catherine-fleming-bruce-in-run-off-election-wciv |access-date=November 10, 2022}}</ref> Angela Geter, chairwoman of the Spartanburg County Democratic Party, also ran in the primary.<ref>{{cite web|last=Conradis|first=Brandon|date=2022-01-21|title=These Senate seats are up for election in 2022|url=https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/590812-these-senate-seats-are-up-for-election-in-2022|access-date=2022-01-29|website=The Hill|language=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite news|agency=Associated Press|date=April 12, 2021|title=Democrat says registration key to ousting SC's Tim Scott|url=https://www.dailyherald.com/article/20210412/news/304129956|access-date=April 12, 2021|website=Daily Herald|author=Kinnard, Meg}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last1=Montgomery |first1=Bob |title=Spartanburg's Dem chair announces bid for U.S. Senator Tim Scott's seat |url=https://www.goupstate.com/story/news/local/2021/05/05/spartanburg-democratic-chair-angela-geter-bid-u-s-senate-tim-scott-seat/4878871001/ |website=GoUpstate |access-date=May 6, 2021|date=May 6, 2021}}</ref>

{{Main|2022 United States Senate election in South Dakota}}

{{See also|List of United States senators from South Dakota|2022 United States House of Representatives election in South Dakota}}
Three-term Republican and U.S. Senate minority whip [[John Thune]] was [[2016 United States Senate election in South Dakota|re-elected in 2016]] with 71.8% of the vote and ran for reelection to a fourth term.<ref name="Thunerunning">{{cite news|url= https://www.politico.com/news/2022/01/08/senate-minority-whip-john-thune-to-run-for-reelection-526794 |title= Senate Minority Whip John Thune to run for reelection |last=Levine|first=Marianne|newspaper=[[Politico]]|date=January 8, 2022|access-date=January 8, 2022}}</ref> Thune had been subject to some backlash from former president Trump and his supporters in the state of South Dakota, leading to speculation of a potential primary challenge.<ref>{{cite news|last=Solender|first=Andrew|date=December 22, 2020|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/andrewsolender/2020/12/22/trump-adds-senate-leader-john-thune-to-list-of-republicans-he-wants-unseated/|title=Trump Adds Senate Leader John Thune To List Of Republicans He Wants Unseated|work=Forbes|access-date=March 22, 2021}}</ref> He defeated Bruce Whalen, an [[Oglala Sioux Tribe|Oglala Sioux]] tribal administrator and former chair of the [[Oglala Lakota County, South Dakota|Oglala Lakota County]] Republican Party in the Republican primary.<ref>{{cite web|title=State Rep. Taffy Howard expected to announce bid for US Congress|website=KEVN|url=https://www.blackhillsfox.com/2021/07/27/state-rep-taffy-howard-expected-announce-bid-us-congress/|date=July 27, 2021|first=Austin|last=Goss}}</ref>

The Democratic candidate was author, navy veteran, and assistant professor of criminal justice at Northern State University, Brian Bengs, who won the primary unopposed.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Dockendorf |first=Randy |title=Democrat Bengs Seeks Major Upset In US Senate Race |url=https://www.yankton.net/community/article_f408317a-f1cc-11ec-891e-67fc5c4f8da7.html |access-date=2022-06-30 |website=Yankton Press & Dakotan |language=en|date=June 22, 2022}}</ref>

{{Main|2022 United States Senate election in Utah}}

{{See also|List of United States senators from Utah|2022 United States House of Representatives elections in Utah}}
Two-term Republican [[Mike Lee]] was [[2016 United States Senate election in Utah|re-elected in 2016]] with 68.2% of the vote. He defeated former state representative [[Becky Edwards (Utah politician)|Becky Edwards]] as well as businessman and political advisor Ally Isom in the Republican primary.<ref name=leeConfirmed>{{cite web |url=https://www.sltrib.com/news/politics/2021/02/09/fundraising-appeal-sen/ |work=Salt Lake Tribune |date=February 9, 2021 |access-date=March 14, 2021 |title=In fundraising appeal, Sen. Mike Lee claims the anti-Trump Lincoln Project is gunning for him next year |first=Bryan |last=Schott}}</ref>

The [[Utah Democratic Party]] declined to field their own candidate against Lee and instead endorsed independent [[Evan McMullin]], a political activist, former Republican, former [[CIA]] operations officer, and [[2016 United States presidential election|2016]] presidential candidate.<ref name="05oct21 politico">{{cite web |last1=Forgey |first1=Quint |title=Evan McMullin announces Utah Senate bid |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2021/10/05/evan-mcmullin-utah-senate-bid-515129 |website=POLITICO |access-date=October 5, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211005210615/https://www.politico.com/news/2021/10/05/evan-mcmullin-utah-senate-bid-515129 |archive-date=October 5, 2021 |language=en |date=October 5, 2021 }}</ref>

Lee won re-election to a third term, defeating McMullin. This was the first Senate election in Utah's history in which there was no [[Democratic Party (United States)|Democratic]] nominee. Lee's performance was the worst for a Republican in a Utah U.S. Senate election since [[1974 United States Senate election in Utah|1974]], while McMullin's was the best ever for an independent in a Utah U.S. Senate race and the best for a non-Republican since [[1976 United States Senate election in Utah|1976]].

{{Main|2022 United States Senate election in Vermont}}

{{See also|List of United States senators from Vermont|2022 United States House of Representatives election in Vermont}}
The most senior senator, an eight-term Democrat and president pro tempore [[Patrick Leahy]], was [[2016 United States Senate election in Vermont|re-elected in 2016]] with 61.3% of the vote. On November 15, 2021, Leahy announced that he would not seek re-election to a ninth term.<ref name="Leahy"/>

Vermont's [[Vermont's at-large congressional district|at-large representative]], Democrat [[Peter Welch]], ran to succeed Leahy.<ref name=VT2022>{{cite web|title=2022 General Election Candidate Listing|url=https://sos.vermont.gov/elections/election-info-resources/candidates/|publisher=Vermont Secretary of State|access-date=August 30, 2022}}</ref>

Former military officer Gerald Malloy was the Republican nominee, having narrowly defeated former [[United States Attorney|United States attorney]] for the [[United States District Court for the District of Vermont|District of Vermont]] [[Christina Nolan]] in the primary.<ref name=VT2022/>

{{Main|2022 United States Senate election in Washington}}

{{See also|List of United States senators from Washington|2022 United States House of Representatives elections in Washington}}
In 2022, the Washington state blanket primary had 18 candidates on the ballot for the U.S. Senate seat.<ref name=primary/> Democrat [[Patty Murray]] ran for re-election to a sixth term.<ref name=MurrayRunning>{{cite news|url=https://mynorthwest.com/2908241/senator-murray-reelection-campaign/|title=Senator Patty Murray announces reelection campaign|work=My Northwest|date=May 17, 2021}}</ref> She won her place on the general election ballot with 52.3% of the vote.<ref name=primary/>
Republican nurse Tiffany Smiley also ran for the Senate seat,<ref>{{cite news|title=Republican Tiffany Smiley says she is running for US Senate|url=https://www.seattletimes.com/seattle-news/republican-tiffany-smiley-says-she-is-running-for-patty-murrays-seat-in-u-s-senate/|website=The Seattle Times|agency=Associated Press|date=April 14, 2021}}</ref> and advanced to the general election after coming in second in the blanket primary with 33.7% of the vote.<ref name=primary/>

Murray defeated Smiley and won re-election to a sixth term in the November 8, 2022 election, receiving 57% of the vote.<ref>{{cite news |last=Gutman |first=David |date=November 8, 2022 |title=Patty Murray defeats Tiffany Smiley in U.S. Senate race |url=https://www.seattletimes.com/seattle-news/politics/patty-murray-leads-tiffany-smiley-in-u-s-senate-race/ |work=[[The Seattle Times]] |accessdate=November 8, 2022}}</ref><ref name=WAofficial2022>{{cite web
|accessdate=2022-11-13| url=https://results.vote.wa.gov/results/20221108/us-senator.html
|title=November 8, 2022 General Election Results: U.S. Senator |date=2022-11-12
|work=Elections & Results |publisher=Washington Secretary of StateElections Division}}</ref>  Smiley conceded the following day.<ref>{{cite web |title=Tiffany Smiley concedes U.S. Senate race to Sen. Patty Murray |url=https://komonews.com/news/local/tiffany-smiley-concedes-us-senate-race-to-sen-patty-murray |website=KOMO News |date=November 9, 2022 |publisher=Sinclair Broadcasting Group |access-date=10 November 2022}}</ref>

{{Main|2022 United States Senate election in Wisconsin}}

{{See also|List of United States senators from Wisconsin|2022 United States House of Representatives elections in Wisconsin}}

Incumbent [[Republican Party of Wisconsin|Republican]] Senator [[Ron Johnson]] won re-election to a third term, defeating the [[Democratic Party of Wisconsin|Democratic]] nominee, [[Lieutenant Governor of Wisconsin|Lieutenant Governor]] [[Mandela Barnes]].<ref>{{cite news |last1=Edelman |first1=Adam |date=November 9, 2022 |title=Ron Johnson defeats Mandela Barnes in Wisconsin Senate race, NBC News projects |work=NBC News |publisher=NBC Universal |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2022-election/ron-johnson-defeats-mandela-barnes-wisconsin-senate-race-nbc-news-proj-rcna54938 |access-date=9 November 2022}}</ref>

In 2016, Johnson had pledged to serve only two terms in the Senate. He reversed this decision in 2022.<ref name="Johnson running">{{cite news |title=Source: U.S. Sen. Ron Johnson to run for reelection |website=wisn.com |url=https://www.wisn.com/article/source-ron-johnson-to-run-for-re-election/38697598 |access-date=January 7, 2022}}</ref> The race was one of the most competitive of the cycle, and it followed considerable Democratic success in recent statewide elections. In [[2018 Wisconsin elections|2018]], Democrats won every statewide contest on the ballot, including the election for the state's [[2018 United States Senate election in Wisconsin|other Senate seat]]. In [[2020 Wisconsin elections|2020]], Democrat [[Joe Biden]] [[2020 United States presidential election in Wisconsin|narrowly carried the state in the presidential election]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Greenwood |first=Max |date=January 1, 2021 |title=Seven Senate Races to Watch in 2022 |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/530880-seven-senate-races-to-watch-in-2022 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210101120928/https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/530880-seven-senate-races-to-watch-in-2022 |archive-date=January 1, 2021 |access-date=January 1, 2021 |website=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |language=en}}</ref>
